=== PROMPT VARIANT ===
r1

=== PROMPT ===
You will be given a competitive programming problem.
You are given an m x n grid. A robot starts at the top-left corner of the grid (0, 0) and wants to reach the bottom-right corner (m - 1, n - 1). The robot can move either right or down at any point in time.
The grid contains a value coins[i][j] in each cell:

If coins[i][j] >= 0, the robot gains that many coins.
If coins[i][j] < 0, the robot encounters a robber, and the robber steals the absolute value of coins[i][j] coins.

The robot has a special ability to neutralize robbers in at most 2 cells on its path, preventing them from stealing coins in those cells.
Note: The robot's total coins can be negative.
Return the maximum profit the robot can gain on the route.
 
Example 1:

Input: coins = [[0,1,-1],[1,-2,3],[2,-3,4]]
Output: 8
Explanation:
An optimal path for maximum coins is:

Start at (0, 0) with 0 coins (total coins = 0).
Move to (0, 1), gaining 1 coin (total coins = 0 + 1 = 1).
Move to (1, 1), where there's a robber stealing 2 coins. The robot uses one neutralization here, avoiding the robbery (total coins = 1).
Move to (1, 2), gaining 3 coins (total coins = 1 + 3 = 4).
Move to (2, 2), gaining 4 coins (total coins = 4 + 4 = 8).


Example 2:

Input: coins = [[10,10,10],[10,10,10]]
Output: 40
Explanation:
An optimal path for maximum coins is:

Start at (0, 0) with 10 coins (total coins = 10).
Move to (0, 1), gaining 10 coins (total coins = 10 + 10 = 20).
Move to (0, 2), gaining another 10 coins (total coins = 20 + 10 = 30).
Move to (1, 2), gaining the final 10 coins (total coins = 30 + 10 = 40).


 
Constraints:

m == coins.length
n == coins[i].length
1 <= m, n <= 500
-1000 <= coins[i][j] <= 1000

Use the following starter code to write the final solution.
```python
class Solution:
    def maximumAmount(self, coins: List[List[int]]) -> int:
        
```

Reason in a commit-implement-recheck way.

A productive rhythm for this task usually looks like this:
- Isolate the decisive constraints, the target complexity, and at most one or two approaches that are still truly plausible.
- If two candidates remain, compare them only on the decisive constraint and commit quickly to the stronger one instead of keeping a wide search open.
- Before coding, keep only a tiny implementation ledger for the chosen plan: main invariant, key state or transition, and the one or two edge cases most likely to fail.
- Move into code as soon as that ledger is clear. After coding starts, keep reasoning attached to the current draft rather than reopening the high-level search.
- After the first complete draft, do one short implementation-check-adjust-check loop on the same draft: test the key edge case, patch locally if needed, and recheck.
- Reopen the main design only if a concrete counterexample breaks the core invariant. Otherwise prefer the local patch and keep closing.
- Once the current draft, invariant, and short recheck still agree, finalize the solution.

Output only the final Python code enclosed in a code block.

Straight DP: dp[k][i][j] = max total coins reaching (i,j) having used k neutralizations (0..2). Transition from top/left. For current cell val v:
- If v >=0: dp[k][i][j] = max(prev[k]) + v (neutralizing no effect; if used maybe same)
- If v <0: 
   not neutralize: dp[k][i][j] = max(prev[k]) + v
   neutralize (if k>0): dp[k][i][j] = max(prev[k-1]) + 0

Pseudo:

class Solution:
    def maximumAmount(self, coins: List[List[int]]) -> int:
        m, n = len(coins), len(coins[0])
        NEG = -10**18
        # previous row dp arrays
        prev0 = [NEG]*n
        prev1 = [NEG]*n
        prev2 = [NEG]*n
        for i in range(m):
            cur0 = [NEG]*n
            cur1 = [NEG]*n
            cur2 = [NEG]*n
            for j in range(n):
                v = coins[i][j]
                # best from top/left for each k
                if i == 0 and j == 0:
                    b0 = b1 = b2 = 0
                    # for neutralize less:
                    # b0_less? for k=1 use b0? Actually for k=1, need prev with at most 0. At start, 0.
                    # for k=2, need prev with at most 1. At start, 0. So b_less for k=1 and k=2 both 0.
                    # But in general b_less[k] = best_prev with at most k-1.
                else:
                    b0 = NEG
                    if i > 0: b0 = max(b0, prev0[j])
                    if j > 0: b0 = max(b0, cur0[j-1])
                    b1 = NEG
                    if i > 0: b1 = max(b1, prev1[j])
                    if j > 0: b1 = max(b1, cur1[j-1])
                    b2 = NEG
                    if i > 0: b2 = max(b2, prev2[j])
                    if j > 0: b2 = max(b2, cur2[j-1])
                if v >= 0:
                    cur0[j] = b0 + v
                    cur1[j] = b1 + v
                    cur2[j] = b2 + v
                else:
                    cur0[j] = b0 + v
                    # k=1
                    # need b0_less: best prev with at most 0
                    if i==0 and j==0:
                        b0_less = 0
                    else:
                        b0_less = NEG
                        if i > 0: b0_less = max(b0_less, prev0[j])
                        if j > 0: b0_less = max(b0_less, cur0[j-1])
                    val1 = max(b1 + v, b0_less)
                    cur1[j] = val1
                    # k=2
                    # need b1_less: best prev with at most 1
                    if i==0 and j==0:
                        b1_less = 0
                    else:
                        b1_less = NEG
                        if i > 0: b1_less = max(b1_less, prev1[j])
                        if j > 0: b1_less = max(b1_less, cur1[j-1])
                    val2 = max(b2 + v, b1_less)
                    cur2[j] = val2
            prev0, prev1, prev2 = cur0, cur1, cur2
        return prev2[n-1]  # at most 2

Pseudo code final:

from typing import List

class Solution:
    def maximumAmount(self, coins: List[List[int]]) -> int:
        m, n = len(coins), len(coins[0])
        NEG = -10**18

        prev0 = [NEG] * n
        prev1 = [NEG] * n
        prev2 = [NEG] * n

        for i in range(m):
            cur0 = [NEG] * n
            cur1 = [NEG] * n
            cur2 = [NEG] * n

            for j in range(n):
                v = coins[i][j]

                if i == 0 and j == 0:
                    b0 = b1 = b2 = 0
                else:
                    b0 = b1 = b2 = NEG
                    if i > 0:
                        b0 = max(b0, prev0[j])
                        b1 = max(b1, prev1[j])
                        b2 = max(b2, prev2[j])
                    if j > 0:
                        b0 = max(b0, cur0[j-1])
                        b1 = max(b1, cur1[j-1])
                        b2 = max(b2, cur2[j-1])

                if v >= 0:
                    cur0[j] = b0 + v
                    cur1[j] = b1 + v
                    cur2[j] = b2 + v
                else:
                    cur0[j] = b0 + v

                    if i == 0 and j == 0:
                        b0_less = 0
                    else:
                        b0_less = NEG
                        if i > 0:
                            b0_less = max(b0_less, prev0[j])
                        if j > 0:
                            b0_less = max(b0_less, cur0[j-1])

                    cur1[j] = max(b1 + v, b0_less)

                    if i == 0 and j == 0:
                        b1_less = 0
                    else:
                        b1_less = NEG
                        if i > 0:
                            b1_less = max(b1_less, prev1[j])
                        if j > 0:
                            b1_less = max(b1_less, cur1[j-1])

                    cur2[j] = max(b2 + v, b1_less)

            prev0, prev1, prev2 = cur0, cur1, cur2

        return prev2[n-1]

We can define DP exactly:
Let dp[k][i][j] = max profit with at most k neutralizations. Recurrence:
dp[k][i][j] = max(
    dp[k][i-1][j] + val(i,j) if not neutralize,
    dp[k][i][j-1] + val(i,j),
    dp[k-1][i-1][j] if val<0 and neutralize,
    dp[k-1][i][j-1] if val<0 and neutralize
) with boundary. For val>=0, neutralization not applicable. Our rolling recurrence matches.

But wait, for val>=0, if we define dp[k] at most k, recurrence should be:
dp[k][i][j] = max(dp[k][i-1][j], dp[k][i][j-1]) + val
No need dp[k-1]. Good.

For val<0:
dp[k][i][j] = max(
   max(dp[k][i-1][j], dp[k][i][j-1]) + val,
   max(dp[k-1][i-1][j], dp[k-1][i][j-1])  # neutralize
)
For k=0, no neutralize. Our code for cur1 uses b1+v and b0_less. b1 is max(dp1 top/left), b0_less max(dp0 top/left). Good.

Let's think if we can simplify by using a 3D DP with dimensions (3, m, n) and fill row-major. That might be easier to verify and less code duplication. But memory okay. Let's compare code length.

3D DP code:
dp = [[[NEG]*n for _ in range(m)] for _ in range(3)]
for i in range(m):
  for j in range(n):
    v=coins[i][j]
    for k in range(3):
      if i==0 and j==0:
         best = 0
      else:
         best = NEG
         if i>0: best=max(best, dp[k][i-1][j])
         if j>0: best=max(best, dp[k][i][j-1])
      if v>=0:
         dp[k][i][j] = best + v
      else:
         if k==0:
            dp[k][i][j] = best + v
         else:
            best_less = NEG
            if i==0 and j==0:
               best_less = 0
            else:
               if i>0: best_less=max(best_less, dp[k-1][i-1][j])
               if j>0: best_less=max(best_less, dp[k-1][i][j-1])
            dp[k][i][j] = max(best + v, best_less)
return dp[2][m-1][n-1]

This is much simpler and less bug-prone. Memory: dp is 3 x m x n. For 500x500, 750k ints. But Python list of lists of lists: outer 3, each m lists of n refs. 3*500*500 = 750k references = 6 MB. Each assigned int object? Initially all NEG references to same int. As we fill, each cell assigned a new int? For each cell, dp[k][i][j] = int. That's 750k int objects ~21 MB. Total ~27 MB plus list overhead ~ maybe 3*500 lists ~ small. 27 MB okay. But LeetCode memory limit maybe 256 MB, fine. Time: loops 250k * 3 = 750k iterations, each with Python operations, fine. Actually 3D DP might be simpler and acceptable. But if m,n 500, 750k int objects, okay. If memory limit 64 MB? 27 MB plus overhead maybe under 64. But Python memory overhead of nested lists: each row list of 500 references (4KB) * 1500 rows = 6MB; int objects 21MB; outer lists small; total ~27MB. The input coins list also memory maybe ~? coins values are Python ints in lists, maybe another 2MB? Actually 250k ints ~7MB plus lists ~2MB. Total maybe 36MB. Under 64? Maybe close but okay. Rolling memory is safer. But 3D code simpler and less likely to have bug. However, LeetCode Python memory limit often 256MB. Fine.

We can also implement rolling with a loop over k to reduce duplication and keep clarity. Let's see.

Rolling with loop over k:
prev = [[NEG]*n for _ in range(3)]
for i in range(m):
    cur = [[NEG]*n for _ in range(3)]
    for j in range(n):
        v = coins[i][j]
        # compute best[3]
        best = [NEG]*3
        if i == 0 and j == 0:
            best = [0,0,0]
        else:
            if i > 0:
                for k in range(3):
                    if prev[k][j] > best[k]: best[k] = prev[k][j]
            if j > 0:
                for k in range(3):
                    if cur[k][j-1] > best[k]: best[k] = cur[k][j-1]
        if v >= 0:
            for k in range(3):
                cur[k][j] = best[k] + v
        else:
            cur[0][j] = best[0] + v
            for k in (1,2):
                # best_less = best previous with at most k-1
                less = NEG
                if i == 0 and j == 0:
                    less = 0
                else:
                    if i > 0:
                        less = max(less, prev[k-1][j])
                    if j > 0:
                        less = max(less, cur[k-1][j-1])
                cur[k][j] = max(best[k] + v, less)
    prev = cur
return prev[2][n-1]

This is concise and memory O(n). But uses lists of lists and inner loops over 3. It might be slightly slower but 250k*3 = 750k, fine. Need be careful: `best = [NEG]*3` then if start `best = [0,0,0]`. Good. For v>=0, `cur[k][j] = best[k] + v` for all k. For v<0, `cur[0][j] = best[0] + v`; for k=1,2, less uses `cur[k-1][j-1]` if j>0. Since we're filling current row left to right, `cur[k-1][j-1]` has been computed. Good. For i>0, `prev[k-1][j]` from previous row. Good.

This rolling loop code is less duplicated and easier to verify. But note: For v>=0, if best[k] is NEG? no. For start, best all 0. Good.

Now, let's decide final code. I'd prefer the rolling loop code for memory and clarity. But need ensure final code is accepted by LeetCode. Let's write it cleanly:

from typing import List

class Solution:
    def maximumAmount(self, coins: List[List[int]]) -> int:
        m, n = len(coins), len(coins[0])
        NEG = -10**18

        prev = [[NEG] * n for _ in range(3)]

        for i in range(m):
            cur = [[NEG] * n for _ in range(3)]

            for j in range(n):
                v = coins[i][j]

                if i == 0 and j == 0:
                    best = [0, 0, 0]
                else:
                    best = [NEG, NEG, NEG]
                    if i > 0:
                        for k in range(3):
                            if prev[k][j] > best[k]:
                                best[k] = prev[k][j]
                    if j > 0:
                        for k in range(3):
                            if cur[k][j - 1] > best[k]:
                                best[k] = cur[k][j - 1]

                if v >= 0:
                    for k in range(3):
                        cur[k][j] = best[k] + v
                else:
                    cur[0][j] = best[0] + v

                    for k in (1, 2):
                        less = NEG
                        if i == 0 and j == 0:
                            less = 0
                        else:
                            if i > 0:
                                if prev[k - 1][j] > less:
                                    less = prev[k - 1][j]
                            if j > 0:
                                if cur[k - 1][j - 1] > less:
                                    less = cur[k - 1][j - 1]
                        cur[k][j] = max(best[k] + v, less)

            prev = cur

        return prev[2][n - 1]

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "at most k" and `best[k]` for k=2 at start v<0 =0, but if later we process another negative and k=2, `less` for k=2 uses prev[1] or cur[1], not prev[2]. So it doesn't "remember" that dp2 at start used only one neutralization and still has one left? Wait, important! Let's examine. We use at-most DP. At start v<0, cur[2]=0. This represents "at most 2 neutralizations used" but actually used 1. It has 1 remaining. But when we move to next cell and want to neutralize it with k=2, our recurrence uses `less` = best previous with at most 1 (cur[1] or prev[1]), not cur[2]. Is that correct? Let's think.

At current cell, if we neutralize it, before current we must have used at most 1 neutralization (so after using current, at most 2). If previous path had used at most2 but actually used1, then before current it used at most1, so it is included in `less` (best previous with at most1). But wait, `less` is max over previous with at most1. If cur[2] at previous cell =0 with used1, and cur[1] at previous cell =0 also (since at most1 and used1), then less includes it. Good. If previous path used at most2 but actually used0, then it's also at most1, included in less. So using `less` with at most k-1 is correct because to neutralize current, total used after current <= k, so before current <= k-1. It doesn't matter if previous dp[k] had unused capacity; that path is also represented in dp[k-1] if it used <=k-1. But is every path with used <=k also represented in dp[k-1]? No, only if used <=k-1. For neutralizing current, we need before current used <=k-1. So we must use dp[k-1], not dp[k]. Good. For not neutralizing current, we can use dp[k] (before current used <=k). If dp[k] path used <=k-1, it's also in dp[k-1], but dp[k] is at least as good. Good.

Now, let's consider if at start v<0, cur[2]=0 and cur[1]=0. At next negative, for k=2, less = cur[1] =0, so neutralize current ->0. This corresponds to using neutralization on start and current (2 used). Good. If we had used cur[2] as less, it would also be0, but for k=2 if previous path used2 and we neutralize current would use3, invalid. But because at-most dp[2] may represent used1, it's tempting to use dp[2] as "remaining capacity" but not valid in general. Our recurrence correctly uses dp[k-1]. Good.

Now, let's maybe think about if there is an even more subtle issue: The DP with at most k and monotonic relies on `best[k] >= best[k-1]`. We proved. But in the rolling loop code, for v<0, `cur[1] = max(best[1] + v, less0)`. `less0` is best previous with at most0. `cur[0] = best[0] + v`. Since `best[1] >= best[0]`, `best[1] + v >= best[0] + v = cur[0]`. So `cur[1] >= cur[0]`. Good. For `cur[2]`, `best[2] >= best[1]`, so `best[2] + v >= best[1] + v`. But `cur[1]` might be `less0` if less0 > best[1]+v. Need `cur[2] >= cur[1]`? Let's check:
cur[1] = max(best[1]+v, less0)
cur[2] = max(best[2]+v, less1)
We know best[2] >= best[1], so best[2]+v >= best[1]+v. Also less1 = best previous with at most1. Is less1 >= cur[1]? cur[1] is current cell with at most1. less1 is previous cell with at most1. Not directly comparable. Need prove cur[2] >= cur[1] for monotonic. Since cur[1] is a valid path with at most1 neutralization, and at most2 includes it, the optimal cur[2] should be at least cur[1]. Does recurrence guarantee? Let's see. cur[1] path either:
- not neutralize current: previous with at most1 (best1) + v. Then previous with at most1 is also a previous with at most2, so best2 >= best1, so best2+v >= best1+v = that path. Thus cur2 via not neutralize >= that path.
- neutralize current: previous with at most0 (less0) + 0. Previous with at most0 is also previous with at most1, so less1 >= less0. Thus cur2 via neutralize >= less0 = that path.
Therefore cur2 >= cur1. Good. So monotonic holds. Our recurrence is correct.

cur[2] = max(best[2]+v, less1)
We know best[2] >= best[1], so best[2]+v >= best[1]+v. Also less1 = best previous with at most1. Is less1 >= cur[1]? cur[1] is current cell with at most1. less1 is previous cell with at most1. Not directly comparable. Need prove cur[2] >= cur[1] for monotonic. Since cur[1] is a valid path with at most1 neutralization, and at most2 includes it, the optimal cur[2] should be at least cur[1]. Does recurrence guarantee? Let's see. cur[1] path either:
- not neutralize current: previous with at most1 (best1) + v. Then previous with at most1 is also a previous with at most2, so best2 >= best1, so best2+v >= best1+v = that path. Thus cur2 via not neutralize >= that path.
- neutralize current: previous with at most0 (less0) + 0. Previous with at most0 is also previous with at most1, so less1 >= less0. Thus cur2 via neutralize >= less0 = that path.
Therefore cur2 >= cur1. Good. So monotonic holds. Our recurrence is correct.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "less1" for k=2 being best previous with at most1, and if previous with at most1 is also a previous with at most2, so best2 >= less1. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "best" for current cell if v>=0 and k=2: cur2 = best2+v. Since best2 >= best1, cur2 >= cur1. Good.

Now, let's maybe think about if there is an even more subtle issue: The DP with at most k and monotonic relies on `best[k] >= best[k-1]`. We proved. But in the rolling loop code, for v<0, `cur[1] = max(best[1] + v, less0)`. `less0` is best previous with at most0. `cur[0] = best[0] + v`. Since `best[1] >= best[0]`, `best[1] + v >= best[0] + v = cur[0]`. So `cur[1] >= cur[0]`. Good. For `cur[2]`, `best[2] >= best[1]`, so `best[2] + v >= best[1] + v`. But `cur[1]` might be `less0` if less0 > best[1]+v. Need `cur[2] >= cur[1]`? Let's check:
cur[1] = max(best[1]+v, less0)
cur[2] = max(best[2]+v, less1)
We know best[2] >= best[1], so best[2]+v >= best[1]+v. Also less1 = best previous with at most1. Is less1 >= cur[1]? cur[1] is current cell with at most1. less1 is previous cell with at most1. Not directly comparable. Need prove cur[2] >= cur[1] for monotonic. Since cur[1] is a valid path with at most1 neutralization, and at most2 includes it, the optimal cur[2] should be at least cur[1]. Does recurrence guarantee? Let's see. cur[1] path either:
- not neutralize current: previous with at most1 (best1) + v. Then previous with at most1 is also a previous with at most2, so best2 >= best1, so best2+v >= best1+v = that path. Thus cur2 via not neutralize >= that path.
- neutralize current: previous with at most0 (less0) + 0. Previous with at most0 is also previous with at most1, so less1 >= less0. Thus cur2 via neutralize >= less0 = that path.
Therefore cur2 >= cur1. Good. So monotonic holds. Our recurrence is correct.

Now, let's maybe think about if there is an even more subtle issue: The DP with at most k and monotonic relies on `best[k] >= best[k-1]`. We proved. But in the rolling loop code, for v<0, `cur[1] = max(best[1] + v, less0)`. `less0` is best previous with at most0. `cur[0] = best[0] + v`. Since `best[1] >= best[0]`, `best[1] + v >= best[0] + v = cur[0]`. So `cur[1] >= cur[0]`. Good. For `cur[2]`, `best[2] >= best[1]`, so `best[2] + v >= best[1] + v`. But `cur[1]` might be `less0` if less0 > best[1]+v. Need `cur[2] >= cur[1]`? Let's check:
cur[1] = max(best[1]+v, less0)
cur[2] = max(best[2]+v, less1)
We know best[2] >= best[1], so best[2]+v >= best[1]+v. Also less1 = best previous with at most1. Is less1 >= cur[1]? cur[1] is current cell with at most1. less1 is previous cell with at most1. Not directly comparable. Need prove cur[2] >= cur[1] for monotonic. Since cur[1] is a valid path with at most1 neutralization, and at most2 includes it, the optimal cur[2] should be at least cur[1]. Does recurrence guarantee? Let's see. cur[1] path either:
- not neutralize current: previous with at most1 (best1) + v. Then previous with at most1 is also a previous with at most2, so best2 >= best1, so best2+v >= best1+v = that path. Thus cur2 via not neutralize >= that path.
- neutralize current: previous with at most0 (less0) + 0. Previous with at most0 is also previous with at most1, so less1 >= less0. Thus cur2 via neutralize >= less0 = that path.
Therefore cur2 >= cur1. Good. So monotonic holds. Our recurrence is correct.

Now, let's maybe think about if there is an even more subtle issue: The DP with at most k and monotonic relies on `best[k] >= best[k-1]`. We proved. But in the rolling loop code, for v<0, `cur[1] = max(best[1] + v, less0)`. `less0` is best previous with at most0. `cur[0] = best[0] + v`. Since `best[1] >= best[0]`, `best[1] + v >= best[0] + v = cur[0]`. So `cur[1] >= cur[0]`. Good. For `cur[2]`, `best[2] >= best[1]`, so `best[2] + v >= best[1] + v`. But `cur[1]` might be `less0` if less0 > best[1]+v. Need `cur[2] >= cur[1]`? Let's check:
cur[1] = max(best[1]+v, less0)
cur[2] = max(best[2]+v, less1)
We know best[2] >= best[1], so best[2]+v >= best[1]+v. Also less1 = best previous with at most1. Is less1 >= cur[1]? cur[1] is current cell with at most1. less1 is previous cell with at most1. Not directly comparable. Need prove cur[2] >= cur[1] for monotonic. Since cur[1] is a valid path with at most1 neutralization, and at most2 includes it, the optimal cur[2] should be at least cur[1]. Does recurrence guarantee? Let's see. cur[1] path either:
- not neutralize current: previous with at most1 (best1) + v. Then previous with at most1 is also a previous with at most2, so best2 >= best1, so best2+v >= best1+v = that path. Thus cur2 via not neutralize >= that path.
- neutralize current: previous with at most0 (less0) + 0. Previous with at most0 is also previous with at most1, so less1 >= less0. Thus cur2 via neutralize >= less0 = that path.
Therefore cur2 >= cur1. Good. So monotonic holds. Our recurrence is correct.

Now, let's maybe think about if there is an even more subtle issue: The DP with at most k and monotonic relies on `best[k] >= best[k-1]`. We proved. But in the rolling loop code, for v<0, `cur[1] = max(best[1] + v, less0)`. `less0` is best previous with at most0. `cur[0] = best[0] + v`. Since `best[1] >= best[0]`, `best[1] + v >= best[0] + v = cur[0]`. So `cur[1] >= cur[0]`. Good. For `cur[2]`, `best[2] >= best[1]`, so `best[2] + v >= best[1] + v`. But `cur[1]` might be `less0` if less0 > best[1]+v. Need `cur[2] >= cur[1]`? Let's check:
cur[1] = max(best[1]+v, less0)
cur[2] = max(best[2]+v, less1)
We know best[2] >= best[1], so best[2]+v >= best[1]+v. Also less1 = best previous with at most1. Is less1 >= cur[1]? cur[1] is current cell with at most1. less1 is previous cell with at most1. Not directly comparable. Need prove cur[2] >= cur[1] for monotonic. Since cur[1] is a valid path with at most1 neutralization, and at most2 includes it, the optimal cur[2] should be at least cur[1]. Does recurrence guarantee? Let's see. cur[1] path either:
- not neutralize current: previous with at most1 (best1) + v. Then previous with at most1 is also a previous with at most2, so best2 >= best1, so best2+v >= best1+v = that path. Thus cur2 via not neutralize >= that path.
- neutralize current: previous with at most0 (less0) + 0. Previous with at most0 is also previous with at most1, so less1 >= less0. Thus cur2 via neutralize >= less0 = that path.
Therefore cur2 >= cur1. Good. So monotonic holds. Our recurrence is correct.

cur[2] = max(best[2]+v, less1)
We know best[2] >= best[1], so best[2]+v >= best[1]+v. Also less1 = best previous with at most1. Is less1 >= cur[1]? cur[1] is current cell with at most1. less1 is previous cell with at most1. Not directly comparable. Need prove cur[2] >= cur[1] for monotonic. Since cur[1] is a valid path with at most1 neutralization, and at most2 includes it, the optimal cur[2] should be at least cur[1]. Does recurrence guarantee? Let's see. cur[1] path either:
- not neutralize current: previous with at most1 (best1) + v. Then previous with at most1 is also a previous with at most2, so best2 >= best1, so best2+v >= best1+v = that path. Thus cur2 via not neutralize >= that path.
- neutralize current: previous with at most0 (less0) + 0. Previous with at most0 is also previous with at most1, so less1 >= less0. Thus cur2 via neutralize >= less0 = that path.
Therefore cur2 >= cur1. Good. So monotonic holds. Our recurrence is correct.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "less1" for k=2 being best previous with at most1, and if previous with at most1 is also a previous with at most2, so best2 >= less1. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "best" for current cell if v>=0 and k=2: cur2 = best2+v. Since best2 >= best1, cur2 >= cur1. Good.

Now, let's maybe think about if there is an even more subtle issue: The DP with at most k and monotonic relies on `best[k] >= best[k-1]`. We proved. But in the rolling loop code, for v<0, `cur[1] = max(best[1] + v, less0)`. `less0` is best previous with at most0. `cur[0] = best[0] + v`. Since `best[1] >= best[0]`, `best[1] + v >= best[0] + v = cur[0]`. So `cur[1] >= cur[0]`. Good. For `cur[2]`, `best[2] >= best[1]`, so `best[2] + v >= best[1] + v`. But `cur[1]` might be `less0` if less0 > best[1]+v. Need `cur[2] >= cur[1]`? Let's check:
cur[1] = max(best[1]+v, less0)
cur[2] = max(best[2]+v, less1)
We know best[2] >= best[1], so best[2]+v >= best[1]+v. Also less1 = best previous with at most1. Is less1 >= cur[1]? cur[1] is current cell with at most1. less1 is previous cell with at most1. Not directly comparable. Need prove cur[2] >= cur[1] for monotonic. Since cur[1] is a valid path with at most1 neutralization, and at most2 includes it, the optimal cur[2] should be at least cur[1]. Does recurrence guarantee? Let's see. cur[1] path either:
- not neutralize current: previous with at most1 (best1) + v. Then previous with at most1 is also a previous with at most2, so best2 >= best1, so best2+v >= best1+v = that path. Thus cur2 via not neutralize >= that path.
- neutralize current: previous with at most0 (less0) + 0. Previous with at most0 is also previous with at most1, so less1 >= less0. Thus cur2 via neutralize >= less0 = that path.
Therefore cur2 >= cur1. Good. So monotonic holds. Our recurrence is correct.

Now, let's maybe think about if there is an even more subtle issue: The DP with at most k and monotonic relies on `best[k] >= best[k-1]`. We proved. But in the rolling loop code, for v<0, `cur[1] = max(best[1] + v, less0)`. `less0` is best previous with at most0. `cur[0] = best[0] + v`. Since `best[1] >= best[0]`, `best[1] + v >= best[0] + v = cur[0]`. So `cur[1] >= cur[0]`. Good. For `cur[2]`, `best[2] >= best[1]`, so `best[2] + v >= best[1] + v`. But `cur[1]` might be `less0` if less0 > best[1]+v. Need `cur[2] >= cur[1]`? Let's check:
cur[1] = max(best[1]+v, less0)
cur[2] = max(best[2]+v, less1)
We know best[2] >= best[1], so best[2]+v >= best[1]+v. Also less1 = best previous with at most1. Is less1 >= cur[1]? cur[1] is current cell with at most1. less1 is previous cell with at most1. Not directly comparable. Need prove cur[2] >= cur[1] for monotonic. Since cur[1] is a valid path with at most1 neutralization, and at most2 includes it, the optimal cur[2] should be at least cur[1]. Does recurrence guarantee? Let's see. cur[1] path either:
- not neutralize current: previous with at most1 (best1) + v. Then previous with at most1 is also a previous with at most2, so best2 >= best1, so best2+v >= best1+v = that path. Thus cur2 via not neutralize >= that path.
- neutralize current: previous with at most0 (less0) + 0. Previous with at most0 is also previous with at most1, so less1 >= less0. Thus cur2 via neutralize >= less0 = that path.
Therefore cur2 >= cur1. Good. So monotonic holds. Our recurrence is correct.

Now, let's maybe think about if there is an even more subtle issue: The DP with at most k and monotonic relies on `best[k] >= best[k-1]`. We proved. But in the rolling loop code, for v<0, `cur[1] = max(best[1] + v, less0)`. `less0` is best previous with at most0. `cur[0] = best[0] + v`. Since `best[1] >= best[0]`, `best[1] + v >= best[0] + v = cur[0]`. So `cur[1] >= cur[0]`. Good. For `cur[2]`, `best[2] >= best[1]`, so `best[2] + v >= best[1] + v`. But `cur[1]` might be `less0` if less0 > best[1]+v. Need `cur[2] >= cur[1]`? Let's check:
cur[1] = max(best[1]+v, less0)
cur[2] = max(best[2]+v, less1)
We know best[2] >= best[1], so best[2]+v >= best[1]+v. Also less1 = best previous with at most1. Is less1 >= cur[1]? cur[1] is current cell with at most1. less1 is previous cell with at most1. Not directly comparable. Need prove cur[2] >= cur[1] for monotonic. Since cur[1] is a valid path with at most1 neutralization, and at most2 includes it, the optimal cur[2] should be at least cur[1]. Does recurrence guarantee? Let's see. cur[1] path either:
- not neutralize current: previous with at most1 (best1) + v. Then previous with at most1 is also a previous with at most2, so best2 >= best1, so best2+v >= best1+v = that path. Thus cur2 via not neutralize >= that path.
- neutralize current: previous with at most0 (less0) + 0. Previous with at most0 is also previous with at most1, so less1 >= less0. Thus cur2 via neutralize >= less0 = that path.
Therefore cur2 >= cur1. Good. So monotonic holds. Our recurrence is correct.

Now, let's maybe think about if there is an even more subtle issue: The DP with at most k and monotonic relies on `best[k] >= best[k-1]`. We proved. But in the rolling loop code, for v<0, `cur[1] = max(best[1] + v, less0)`. `less0` is best previous with at most0. `cur[0] = best[0] + v`. Since `best[1] >= best[0]`, `best[1] + v >= best[0] + v = cur[0]`. So `cur[1] >= cur[0]`. Good. For `cur[2]`, `best[2] >= best[1]`, so `best[2] + v >= best[1] + v`. But `cur[1]` might be `less0` if less0 > best[1]+v. Need `cur[2] >= cur[1]`? Let's check:
cur[1] = max(best[1]+v, less0)
cur[2] = max(best[2]+v, less1)
We know best[2] >= best[1], so best[2]+v >= best[1]+v. Also less1 = best previous with at most1. Is less1 >= cur[1]? cur[1] is current cell with at most1. less1 is previous cell with at most1. Not directly comparable. Need prove cur[2] >= cur[1] for monotonic. Since cur[1] is a valid path with at most1 neutralization, and at most2 includes it, the optimal cur[2] should be at least cur[1]. Does recurrence guarantee? Let's see. cur[1] path either:
- not neutralize current: previous with at most1 (best1) + v. Then previous with at most1 is also a previous with at most2, so best2 >= best1, so best2+v >= best1+v = that path. Thus cur2 via not neutralize >= that path.
- neutralize current: previous with at most0 (less0) + 0. Previous with at most0 is also previous with at most1, so less1 >= less0. Thus cur2 via neutralize >= less0 = that path.
Therefore cur2 >= cur1. Good. So monotonic holds. Our recurrence is correct.

cur[2] = max(best[2]+v, less1)
We know best[2] >= best[1], so best[2]+v >= best[1]+v. Also less1 = best previous with at most1. Is less1 >= cur[1]? cur[1] is current cell with at most1. less1 is previous cell with at most1. Not directly comparable. Need prove cur[2] >= cur[1] for monotonic. Since cur[1] is a valid path with at most1 neutralization, and at most2 includes it, the optimal cur[2] should be at least cur[1]. Does recurrence guarantee? Let's see. cur[1] path either:
- not neutralize current: previous with at most1 (best1) + v. Then previous with at most1 is also a previous with at most2, so best2 >= best1, so best2+v >= best1+v = that path. Thus cur2 via not neutralize >= that path.
- neutralize current: previous with at most0 (less0) + 0. Previous with at most0 is also previous with at most1, so less1 >= less0. Thus cur2 via neutralize >= less0 = that path.
Therefore cur2 >= cur1. Good. So monotonic holds. Our recurrence is correct.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "less1" for k=2 being best previous with at most1, and if previous with at most1 is also a previous with at most2, so best2 >= less1. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "best" for current cell if v>=0 and k=2: cur2 = best2+v. Since best2 >= best1, cur2 >= cur1. Good.

Now, let's maybe think about if there is an even more subtle issue: The DP with at most k and monotonic relies on `best[k] >= best[k-1]`. We proved. But in the rolling loop code, for v<0, `cur[1] = max(best[1] + v, less0)`. `less0` is best previous with at most0. `cur[0] = best[0] + v`. Since `best[1] >= best[0]`, `best[1] + v >= best[0] + v = cur[0]`. So `cur[1] >= cur[0]`. Good. For `cur[2]`, `best[2] >= best[1]`, so `best[2] + v >= best[1] + v`. But `cur[1]` might be `less0` if less0 > best[1]+v. Need `cur[2] >= cur[1]`? Let's check:
cur[1] = max(best[1]+v, less0)
cur[2] = max(best[2]+v, less1)
We know best[2] >= best[1], so best[2]+v >= best[1]+v. Also less1 = best previous with at most1. Is less1 >= cur[1]? cur[1] is current cell with at most1. less1 is previous cell with at most1. Not directly comparable. Need prove cur[2] >= cur[1] for monotonic. Since cur[1] is a valid path with at most1 neutralization, and at most2 includes it, the optimal cur[2] should be at least cur[1]. Does recurrence guarantee? Let's see. cur[1] path either:
- not neutralize current: previous with at most1 (best1) + v. Then previous with at most1 is also a previous with at most2, so best2 >= best1, so best2+v >= best1+v = that path. Thus cur2 via not neutralize >= that path.
- neutralize current: previous with at most0 (less0) + 0. Previous with at most0 is also previous with at most1, so less1 >= less0. Thus cur2 via neutralize >= less0 = that path.
Therefore cur2 >= cur1. Good. So monotonic holds. Our recurrence is correct.

Let's craft final code. Use rolling DP with 3 states. Need include `from typing import List`. Code:

```python
from typing import List

class Solution:
    def maximumAmount(self, coins: List[List[int]]) -> int:
        m, n = len(coins), len(coins[0])
        NEG = -10**18

        prev = [[NEG] * n for _ in range(3)]

        for i in range(m):
            cur = [[NEG] * n for _ in range(3)]

            for j in range(n):
                v = coins[i][j]

                if i == 0 and j == 0:
                    best = [0, 0, 0]
                else:
                    best = [NEG, NEG, NEG]
                    if i > 0:
                        for k in range(3):
                            if prev[k][j] > best[k]:
                                best[k] = prev[k][j]
                    if j > 0:
                        for k in range(3):
                            if cur[k][j - 1] > best[k]:
                                best[k] = cur[k][j - 1]

                if v >= 0:
                    for k in range(3):
                        cur[k][j] = best[k] + v
                else:
                    cur[0][j] = best[0] + v

                    for k in (1, 2):
                        less = NEG
                        if i == 0 and j == 0:
                            less = 0
                        else:
                            if i > 0:
                                if prev[k - 1][j] > less:
                                    less = prev[k - 1][j]
                            if j > 0:
                                if cur[k - 1][j - 1] > less:
                                    less = cur[k - 1][j - 1]
                        cur[k][j] = max(best[k] + v, less)

            prev = cur

        return prev[2][n - 1]
```

Need ensure no bug. Let's double-check with start negative and v<0: best [0,0,0], cur0=v, less=0, cur1=max(v,0)=0, cur2=0. Good.

For non-start negative, less for k=1 uses prev0/cur0; for k=2 uses prev1/cur1. Good.

For nonnegative, no neutralization. Good.

Return prev[2][n-1]. Good.

Now, final answer should be code block only.</think>```python
from typing import List

class Solution:
    def maximumAmount(self, coins: List[List[int]]) -> int:
        m, n = len(coins), len(coins[0])
        NEG = -10**18

        prev = [[NEG] * n for _ in range(3)]

        for i in range(m):
            cur = [[NEG] * n for _ in range(3)]

            for j in range(n):
                v = coins[i][j]

                if i == 0 and j == 0:
                    best = [0, 0, 0]
                else:
                    best = [NEG, NEG, NEG]
                    if i > 0:
                        for k in range(3):
                            if prev[k][j] > best[k]:
                                best[k] = prev[k][j]
                    if j > 0:
                        for k in range(3):
                            if cur[k][j - 1] > best[k]:
                                best[k] = cur[k][j - 1]

                if v >= 0:
                    for k in range(3):
                        cur[k][j] = best[k] + v
                else:
                    cur[0][j] = best[0] + v

                    for k in (1, 2):
                        less = NEG
                        if i == 0 and j == 0:
                            less = 0
                        else:
                            if i > 0:
                                if prev[k - 1][j] > less:
                                    less = prev[k - 1][j]
                            if j > 0:
                                if cur[k - 1][j - 1] > less:
                                    less = cur[k - 1][j - 1]

                        cur[k][j] = max(best[k] + v, less)

            prev = cur

        return prev[2][n - 1]
```
